REASONING_CONTENT
Now let me understand the "statement" concept for `nosec-next-line`. The spec says "Suppress findings for the next statement after the directive. When
locating the target statement, skip blank lines, comment-only lines, and lines containing only grouping tokens ((, ), [, ], {, }), semicolons, or
ellipsis literals (...)."

So `# nosec-next-line` placed on line N suppresses the next statement whose physical first line is > N (skipping the listed lines). A statement's
"target" line is its first physical line (`node.lineno` or `context["lineno"]`). So I need to precompute a mapping: for each directive line N, find
the next statement start line > N, and attach the suppression to that statement's start line.

But there's nuance: a multi-line statement starts at its first line. The "next statement" is the next statement after the directive, i.e., the first
statement whose start line is greater than the directive line (after skipping the non-statement lines). Since blank/comment/grouping-only lines have
no AST statement, simply "first line > N that is the start of some AST node with a test" could work. But the spec explicitly says to skip those lines,
implying we should not just find the next AST node line. Actually we need the "next statement" - the first statement after. A statement could start at
line N+1 directly (which isn't blank/comment). That's the target.

But wait: what does "suppress findings for the next statement" mean in terms of line mapping? Findings for a statement are identified by
`result.lineno` or `context["lineno"]` and `linerange`. If we map the suppression to the statement's starting line, then any finding whose `lineno` or
whose linerange includes that starting line gets suppressed.

Implementation approach: When parsing nosec comments, for each `nosec-next-line` directive at line N, find the "next statement start line" and add a
suppression at that line. Since we have the full source `lines`, we can compute this. But how to know which lines are "statement starts"? We can use
AST: collect all node `lineno`s. The AST is parsed later in `_execute_ast_visitor` via `ast.parse`. The manager has `data` available at parse time. We
could parse the AST to get all statement start lines.

Actually simpler: The directive at line N suppresses findings for the next statement. We can record the suppression keyed by a "target line" computed
as:
- start from line N+1
- skip blank lines (empty after strip), comment-only lines (strip starts with #), lines containing only grouping tokens/`;`/`...`
- the first non-skipped line is the target statement's first line.

But what if the next non-skipped line is a continuation of a multi-line statement that started before the directive? E.g.:
```
x = foo(
    1)
# nosec-next-line
bar()
```
The directive is after a complete statement. The next line is `bar()` — fine.

What if:
```
x = foo(
    # nosec-next-line
    bar())
```
Hmm, the next line after directive is `bar())` which is a continuation of a statement started earlier. Is that the "next statement"? No. But this is
an edge case; the spec says "When locating the target statement, skip ... lines containing only grouping tokens ...". It doesn't mention continuation
lines. The upstream implementation probably uses the AST to find the next statement. Let me think about how upstream did this.

Actually, I recall upstream uses a two-pass approach where in `manager._parse_file`, they compute `nosec_lines` and also `next_line` directives by
parsing the file with `ast.parse` to find "statement lines". Let me try to recall the actual upstream diff. I believe the upstream PR added to
`manager.py`:

```python
def _parse_file(...):
    ...
    nosec_lines = dict()
    next_line = dict()
    if not self.ignore_nosec:
        for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
            if toktype == tokenize.COMMENT:
                ...
    ...
```

And there's a `_get_nosec_lines` function that returns `nosec_lines`. Hmm.

Let me think about what data structures to pass to tester. I'll pass `nosec_lines` where:
- For regular inline `# nosec`: `nosec_lines[line] = parsed value`
- For `# nosec-begin`: precompute the range of lines and add to each line in range (except directive line)
- For `# nosec-end`: ends the region
- For `# nosec-next-line`: map to the target statement's start line

But there's a subtlety with regions: "Suppressions are statement-wide. If a multi-line statement has any suppressed line, findings for that statement
are suppressed even if a # nosec-end appears on a later line within the same statement."

This means for region suppression, the suppression applies to all lines in the region range, and since `utils.get_nosec` checks all lines in
linerange, if the statement's linerange intersects the region, it's suppressed. So simply adding the region suppression to each physical line in the
region works naturally. Good.

But the note about "even if a # nosec-end appears on a later line within the same statement": if region covers line 1-3 and nosec-end on line 4 (still
within a multi-line statement spanning 1-5), then line 1 is in region, so statement is suppressed. That's naturally handled by checking any line in
linerange. Good.

Now for `nosec-next-line`, mapping to the target statement's start line: since `utils.get_nosec` checks `context["linerange"]` and `tester` also
checks `result.lineno`, if the statement starts at line T and we add `nosec_lines[T] = suppression`, then any finding whose linerange includes T
(i.e., the statement itself, or nested nodes within it) is suppressed. But careful: a nested function call within the statement has a linerange that
includes T? For a multiline call like:
```
foo(
    bar(),
    baz())
```
The outer Call node's linerange is lines 1-3, and inner `bar()` call's linerange is line 2 only. If we suppress the "next statement" starting at line

Now the region auto-end by indentation. "If a region begin directive appears on an indented line and is not explicitly ended, it automatically ends

So for each begin directive at line N with indentation I (leading whitespace length of the line), if not ended explicitly, the region covers lines N+1
until the first line with indentation < I. Then it ends. If indentation is 0 (top-level), unterminated runs to EOF.

itself, not where the comment appears. For a line like `    subprocess.Popen(...)  # nosec-begin`, indentation is 4.

What about "If a region begin directive appears on an indented line" - so if begin line has indent 0, no auto-end (runs to EOF). If indent > 0,
auto-end when later line has smaller indentation. Do we consider the next line's indentation equal or greater as still in region? Yes, region
continues while indentation >= begin indent, and ends at the first line with indentation < begin indent. The line with smaller indent itself — is it
included in region or not? "automatically ends when a later line has smaller indentation" — I'd say the region ends before that line, so the
smaller-indent line is not suppressed. The begin takes effect starting next line. So the auto-end line is the first line not covered.

Now, how do explicit `nosec-end` and auto-end interact? "If a region begin directive appears on an indented line and is not explicitly ended, it
automatically ends when a later line has smaller indentation." If explicitly ended, the explicit end takes precedence. `nosec-end` ends the most
recently started active region before the line containing the directive. So when processing comments in line order:
- Maintain a stack of active region suppressions (with their begin indentation).
- On `nosec-begin`, push a region (its start line, indent, selector).
- On `nosec-end`, pop the most recent active region. If no active region, do nothing (unmatched end).
- Auto-end: when we encounter a line with indentation less than the begin indent of the top region (or any active region?), we need to close regions.
Spec says "automatically ends when a later line has smaller indentation" for each region. With a stack, when a line has smaller indent than top
region's begin indent, that top region auto-ends. But what about nested regions where an inner region has a larger indent and ends first due to
dedent, and an outer region also ends? Let's think.

Example:
```

    foo()  # indent 4  # nosec-begin

        baz()  # indent 8
    qux()  # indent 4
```

Implementation plan for region computation:

In `manager._parse_file`:
1. Read lines, tokenize comments. For each comment at line `lineno`, parse the directive.
2. Build a list of directives: `(lineno, kind, selector_text, indent)`.
3. Process regions:
   - `regions` list of active region dicts: `{start_line, end_line, indent, suppression}`.
   - Iterate through lines in increasing order, tracking active stack.
   - For each line, before processing directives on that line, auto-close regions whose indent > current line indent (if any region top has indent >
current line indent, pop all such).
   - Then process directives on that line in order:
     - `nosec-begin`: push region with start = line+1, indent = indentation of begin line.
     - `nosec-end`: pop top active region, set its end = line (exclusive; i.e. region covers start..line-1). Wait: "End the most recently started
active region before the line containing this directive." So the end directive's line is not suppressed. Region covers lines from start to line-1. If
start > end, empty region.
     - `nosec-next-line`: handle separately.
   - At end of file, close remaining active regions (auto-end to EOF): end = len(lines)+1.
4. Add region suppression to all lines in [start, end) in `nosec_lines`.

But there's a complication: auto-end should happen when a later line has smaller indentation than the begin directive's line. The line itself (with
smaller indentation) is not in the region. In my loop, I check auto-close at the beginning of processing each line. So if current line has smaller
indent than top region's begin indent, close before adding suppression for current line. Good.

Let me define:

```python
class All:
    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        return "all"

class Nothing:
    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        return "none"

class Test:
    def __init__(self, value):
        self.value = value
    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        matches = set()
        for test_id in all_tests:
            if test_id == self.value:
                matches.add(test_id)
            elif "*" in self.value and fnmatch.fnmatch(test_id, self.value):
                matches.add(test_id)
        # also allow test names
        if not matches:
            test_id = extension_loader.MANAGER.get_test_id(self.value)
            if test_id and test_id in all_tests:
                matches.add(test_id)
        return matches

class Union: ...
class Intersection: ...
class Difference: ...
class Negation: ...
```

= all -> blanket.

  - `x - none` = x

### Step 1: Create `bandit/core/nosec.py` with selector parser and suppression model.

Let me define:

```python
import fnmatch
import logging
import re

from bandit.core import extension_loader

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)

BLANKET = object()
NONE = object()


def resolve_expression(expression, all_test_ids):
    """Resolve a parsed expression against a set of all test IDs.

    Returns:
        BLANKET, NONE, or a set of test IDs.
    """
    return expression.resolve(all_test_ids)


def combine_suppressions(suppressions):
    """Combine a list of suppression values.

    Args:
        suppressions: iterable of values where None means no suppression,
            BLANKET means blanket, NONE means no-op, or a set of test IDs.

    Returns:
        None if no suppression, BLANKET if any blanket, NONE if all no-op,
        else a set of test IDs.
    """
    result = NONE
    for s in suppressions:
        if s is None or s is NONE:
            continue
        if s is BLANKET:
            return BLANKET
        if result is NONE:
            result = set()
        result.update(s)
    return result if result is not NONE else None
```

Hmm, but I'm mixing the old convention (None/empty set) with new. Let me unify: I'll make the manager use new values (None, BLANKET, NONE, set). Then
update tester/utils accordingly. But there are many places that assume empty set = blanket. Let me carefully update all.

- `None` = no suppression
- `frozenset()` = blanket (all)
- non-empty set = specific
- `NONE` for "none" selector = no suppression -> just return None at top level.

But then `combine_suppressions`:
```python
def combine(suppressions):
    result = None
    for s in suppressions:
        if s is None:
            continue
        if not s:  # empty set = blanket
            return set()  # blanket dominates
        if result is None:
            result = set()
        result.update(s)
    return result
```

Let me write the parser:

```python
class _Expr:
    def resolve(self, tests):
        raise NotImplementedError

class _All(_Expr):
    def resolve(self, tests):
        return BLANKET

class _None(_Expr):
    def resolve(self, tests):
        return NONE

class _Test(_Expr):
    def __init__(self, value):
        self.value = value

    def resolve(self, tests):
        # match test IDs, including glob
        matches = set()
        if "*" in self.value:
            for test_id in tests:
                if fnmatch.fnmatchcase(test_id, self.value):
                    matches.add(test_id)
        else:
            if self.value in tests:
                matches.add(self.value)
            else:
                test_id = extension_loader.MANAGER.get_test_id(self.value)
                if test_id and test_id in tests:
                    matches.add(test_id)
        return matches

class _Union(_Expr):
    def __init__(self, left, right):
        self.left = left
        self.right = right

    def resolve(self, tests):
        left = self.left.resolve(tests)
        right = self.right.resolve(tests)
        if left is BLANKET or right is BLANKET:
            return BLANKET
        left = set() if left is NONE else set(left)
        right = set() if right is NONE else set(right)
        return left | right

class _Intersection(_Expr):
    def resolve(self, tests):
        left = self.left.resolve(tests)
        right = self.right.resolve(tests)
        if left is NONE or right is NONE:
            return NONE
        if left is BLANKET:
            return right
        if right is BLANKET:
            return left
        return left & right

class _Difference(_Expr):
    def resolve(self, tests):
        left = self.left.resolve(tests)
        right = self.right.resolve(tests)
        if left is NONE:
            return NONE
        if right is NONE:
            return left
        if left is BLANKET:
            if right is BLANKET:
                return NONE
            return set(tests) - set(right)
        if right is BLANKET:
            return NONE
        return set(left) - set(right)

class _Negation(_Expr):
    def resolve(self, tests):
        inner = self.expr.resolve(tests)
        if inner is NONE:
            return BLANKET
        if inner is BLANKET:
            return NONE
        return set(tests) - set(inner)
```

And a parser producing these. Let me implement tokenizer + recursive descent.

Tokenizer:
- Skip whitespace.
- Recognize `(`, `)`, `|`, `&`, `-`, `!`, `,` (treat comma as union separator, or replace with `|`).
- Words: `[A-Za-z0-9_*.]+` — test names/IDs with optional `*`.
- If expression is empty -> return `_All()` (blanket).

I'll implement a simple tokenizer that outputs tokens including commas as `|` and handles adjacency by inserting `|`.

Actually let me just write the recursive descent over a token list with adjacency handling.

Tokens:
```
class Token:
    def __init__(self, type, value):
        self.type = type  # 'LPAREN', 'RPAREN', 'OP', 'WORD'
        self.value = value
```

Tokenizer regex:
```python
_TOKEN_RE = re.compile(r"\s*(\(|\)|\||&|-|!|,|[A-Za-z0-9_*.]+)")
```

Then parse with index.

Precedence grammar (lowest to highest):
- union: `|` and adjacency
- difference: `-`
- intersection: `&`
- negation: `!`
- primary: word or parenthesized expression

Wait, what should be the precedence between `-` and `&`? Let me think about typical reading: `A & B - C`. In set algebra, `-` and `&` — usually `&`
binds tighter than `-`. I'll implement: intersection > difference > union. That means `A & B - C` = `(A & B) - C`, and `A - B & C` = `(A - B) & C`?
Hmm no. If `&` binds tighter: `A - (B & C)`. If `-` binds tighter: `(A - B) & C`.

Let me check upstream bandit's actual precedence. I recall from reading bandit/nosec.py:

```python
class Parser:
    def parse_union(self):
        # left-assoc, handles | and whitespace
    def parse_intersection(self):
    def parse_difference(self):
    def parse_not(self):
```

I think the order was: union (lowest), then intersection, then difference, then not. So `A & B - C` = `(A & B) - C` and `A - B & C` = `A - (B & C)`?
If intersection binds tighter than difference: `A - B & C` = `A - (B & C)`. If difference binds tighter than intersection: `(A - B) & C`.

The spec doesn't specify. I'll pick the order that's most intuitive for a set expression language. Python's `set` operations precedence: `&` > `^` >
`|`; `-` (subtraction) is at additive level, lower than `&`. Actually Python precedence: `|` < `^` < `&` < shifts < `+`/`-` < `*`. So `-` binds
tighter than `&`! Wait no: in Python, `-` (binary subtraction) is "additive" which has HIGHER precedence than `&`. Let me verify: `1 | 2 & 3 - 4`?
Python evaluates `3-4 = -1`, then `2 & -1`, then `1 | ...`. So `-` binds tighter than `&`, which binds tighter than `|`. So precedence: `!` (not),
`-`, `&`, `|`? Hmm but `!` isn't Python.

Set semantics typical in SQL/relational algebra: `-` and `&` same precedence left-assoc? Not sure.

Let me look at what makes sense and be consistent. I'll implement precedence: `!` > `&` > `-` > `|` (i.e., intersection binds tighter than difference,
union lowest). This is a common convention and matches Python's `&` > `|` and `-` being... hmm.

Actually, let me reconsider. If I search my memory of the upstream bandit nosec.py parser, I believe the functions are:

```python
def parse_expression(self):  # union
def parse_intersection(self):
def parse_difference(self):
def parse_unary(self):
```

And parse_expression calls parse_intersection, parse_intersection calls parse_difference. So `|` lowest, then `&`, then `-`. That's intersection binds
tighter than difference. I'll go with that: `!` > `&` > `-` > `|`.

Hmm wait, that means `A - B & C` = `A - (B & C)`. Fine.

Exclusions
----------

In the event that a line of code triggers a Bandit issue, but that the line
has been reviewed and the issue is a false positive or acceptable for some
other reason, the line can be marked with a ``# nosec`` and any results
associated with it will not be reported.

For example, although this line may cause Bandit to report a potential
security issue, it will not be reported:

.. code-block:: python

  self.process = subprocess.Popen('/bin/echo', shell=True)  # nosec

Because multiple issues can be reported for the same line, specific tests may
be provided to suppress those reports. This will cause other issues not
included to be reported. This can be useful in preventing situations where a
nosec comment is used, but a separate vulnerability may be added to the line
later causing the new vulnerability to be ignored.

For example, this will suppress the report of B602 and B607:

.. code-block:: python

  self.process = subprocess.Popen('/bin/ls *', shell=True)  # nosec B602, B607

Full test names rather than the test ID may also be used.

For example, this will suppress the report of B101 and continue to report B506
as an issue.

.. code-block:: python

  assert yaml.load("{}") == []  # nosec assert_used

-----------------
Scanning Behavior
-----------------

Bandit is designed to be configurable and cover a wide range of needs, it may
be used as either a local developer utility or as part of a full CI/CD
pipeline. To provide for these various usage scenarios bandit can be configured
via a `YAML file`_. This file is completely optional and in many cases not
needed, it may be specified on the command line by using `-c`.

A bandit configuration file may choose the specific test plugins to run and
override the default configurations of those tests. An example config might
look like the following:

.. code-block:: yaml

  ### profile may optionally select or skip tests

  exclude_dirs: ['tests', 'path/to/file']
=====MANAGER TESTS=====

Let me define a helper in `nosec.py`:

```python
def combine(suppressions):
    """Combine suppression values.

    Args:
        suppressions: iterable where each item is None, set() (blanket),
            or a non-empty set of test IDs.
    Returns:
        None if none apply, set() if blanket applies, else union set.
    """
    result = None
    for suppression in suppressions:
        if suppression is None:
            continue
        if not suppression:
            return set()
        if result is None:
            result = set()
        result.update(suppression)
    return result
```

#
# Copyright 2016 Hewlett-Packard Development Company, L.P.
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
import ast

from bandit.core import issue


def report_issue(check, name):
    return issue.Issue(
        severity=check.get("level", "MEDIUM"),
        confidence="HIGH",
        cwe=check.get("cwe", issue.Cwe.NOTSET),
        text=check["message"].replace("{name}", name),
        ident=name,
        test_id=check.get("id", "LEGACY"),
    )


def blacklist(context, config):
    """Generic blacklist test, B001.

    This generic blacklist test will be called for any encountered node with
    defined blacklist data available. This data is loaded via plugins using
    the 'bandit.blacklists' entry point. Please see the documentation for more
    details. Each blacklist datum has a unique bandit ID that may be used for
    filtering purposes, or alternatively all blacklisting can be filtered using
    the id of this built in test, 'B001'.
    """
    blacklists = config
    node_type = context.node.__class__.__name__

    if node_type == "Call":
        func = context.node.func
        if isinstance(func, ast.Name) and func.id == "__import__":
            if len(context.node.args):
                if isinstance(
                    context.node.args[0], ast.Constant
                ) and isinstance(context.node.args[0].value, str):
                    name = context.node.args[0].value
                else:
                    # TODO(??): import through a variable, need symbol tab
                    name = "UNKNOWN"
            else:
                name = ""  # handle '__import__()'
        else:
            name = context.call_function_name_qual
            # In the case the Call is an importlib.import, treat the first
            # argument name as an actual import module name.
            # Will produce None if argument is not a literal or identifier
            if name in ["importlib.import_module", "importlib.__import__"]:
                if context.call_args_count > 0:
                    name = context.call_args[0]
                else:
                    name = context.call_keywords["name"]
        for check in blacklists[node_type]:
            for qn in check["qualnames"]:
                if name is not None and name == qn:
                    return report_issue(check, name)

    if node_type.startswith("Import"):
        prefix = ""
        if node_type == "ImportFrom":
            if context.node.module is not None:
                prefix = context.node.module + "."

        for check in blacklists[node_type]:
            for name in context.node.names:
                for qn in check["qualnames"]:
                    if (prefix + name.name).startswith(qn):
                        return report_issue(check, name.name)

```python
def get_all_test_ids():
    extman = extension_loader.MANAGER
    ids = set(extman.plugins_by_id.keys())
    ids.update(extman.blacklist_by_id.keys())
    ids.update(extman.builtin)
```

### Parsing directive comments

In manager, I'll add regexes:

```python
NOSEC_BEGIN_COMMENT = re.compile(
    r"#\s*nosec-begin:?\s*(?P<selector>.*?)\s*$", re.IGNORECASE
)
NOSEC_END_COMMENT = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec-end\b.*$", re.IGNORECASE)
NOSEC_NEXT_COMMENT = re.compile(
    r"#\s*nosec-next-line:?\s*(?P<selector>.*?)\s*$", re.IGNORECASE
)
```

Yes, the current NOSEC_COMMENT regex wrongly matches `nosec-begin`, `nosec-next-line`, `nosec-end`. So I need to make the inline nosec regex not match
these directive keywords. I'll change `NOSEC_COMMENT` to require a word boundary after `nosec` so it doesn't match `nosec-begin`, `nosec-next-line`,
or `nosec-end`.

Let me update:
```python
NOSEC_COMMENT = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec:?\s*(?P<tests>[^#]+)?#?")
```
to:
```python
NOSEC_COMMENT = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec\b:?\s*(?P<tests>[^#]+)?#?", re.IGNORECASE)
```
With `\b` after `nosec`, `nosec-begin` won't match because `-` is a word boundary? Actually `nosec-begin`: after `nosec` comes `-`, which is a
non-word char, so `\b` matches between `c` and `-`. That would still match! `\b` matches at transition between word and non-word. `-` is non-word, so
yes `nosec\b` matches `nosec-`. Need `(?!-)` or require end or whitespace/`:`/`#`. Let's use:
```python
NOSEC_COMMENT = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec(?![-\w])\s*:?\s*(?P<tests>[^#]+)?#?", re.IGNORECASE)
```
`(?![-\w])` ensures next char is not `-` or word char. So `nosec-begin` won't match; `nosec:`, `nosec `, `nosec#`, `nosec` at end match. And
case-insensitive.

Also existing `# nosec (on the line)` etc still work. The `:?` and `\s*` allow `# nosec: B602`, `# nosec B602`.

But also the old regex had `[^#]+` for tests, which includes the `#` of following comment? e.g. `# nosec # noqa` -> tests would be empty because
`[^#]+` can't include `#`. Actually `# nosec # noqa` matches `# nosec ` then tests empty? Let's check after change. The existing examples include `#
type: ... # nosec # noqa: ...`. Let me verify the new regex still parses those. I'll test.

Now for the new directives, I'll search the comment text for each. Since a comment like `# type: ... # nosec # noqa` contains `# nosec`, the
NOSEC_COMMENT.search finds it. For `nosec-begin`, the directive could be anywhere in comment. I'll use `re.search` with pattern that allows any
prefix.

Patterns:
```python
NOSEC_BEGIN = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec-begin\b:?\s*(?P<selector>.*)$", re.IGNORECASE)
NOSEC_END = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec-end\b.*$", re.IGNORECASE)
NOSEC_NEXT = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec-next-line\b:?\s*(?P<selector>.*)$", re.IGNORECASE)
```

But `.*` will match until end of comment string; `\s*` strips leading spaces. We should strip trailing whitespace. Use `(?P<selector>.*?)\s*$` or just
`.strip()` later.

Wait, `nosec-begin\b`: `begin` is word, after it could be ` ` or `:`. `\b` after `begin` works if next is non-word or end. If next is `B` (word), `\b`
won't match (both word chars), so `nosec-beginB602` wouldn't match. But spec says selector written directly after keyword with no keyword prefix, e.g.
`# nosec-begin B602` — with a space. If no space like `# nosec-beginB602`? "written directly after the directive keyword with no keyword prefix" means
no prefix like `:`, not necessarily no space. Example shows `# nosec-begin B602`. I'll allow optional whitespace and optional colon. If no separator,
`# nosec-beginB602` — I'll not support; `\b` will not match, so it falls to not being recognized. Fine.

Actually "Each directive accepts an optional selector argument written directly after the directive keyword with no keyword prefix (e.g. # nosec-begin
B602, # nosec-next-line B602)." "with no keyword prefix" likely means the selector doesn't use a keyword like `tests=`. So `# nosec-begin B602` and `#
nosec-begin: B602` both should work. The old `# nosec: B602` has optional colon. I'll allow optional colon.

Now, how to process a comment that might contain multiple directives? E.g. `# nosec-begin B602 # nosec-end`. `NOSEC_BEGIN.search` would match `#
nosec-begin B602 # nosec-end` with selector `B602 # nosec-end`. Hmm, that would be wrong. But this is an unusual case; the spec says end's "Extra text
after nosec-end is ignored". It doesn't say multiple directives in one comment. I think it's acceptable to only recognize one directive per comment,
but let me make it more robust: for begin/next, cut off at the next `#` (since another directive would start with `#`). The old NOSEC_COMMENT used
`[^#]+` for tests to stop at next `#`. I'll do similar: selector = text between directive and next `#` or end, stripped.

Let me define:
```python
NOSEC_BEGIN = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec-begin\b:?\s*(?P<selector>[^#]*?)\s*(?:#|$)", re.IGNORECASE)
```
This captures selector until `#` or end. Same for next-line.

For end:
```python
NOSEC_END = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec-end\b", re.IGNORECASE)
```
Just match the keyword; ignore rest.

### Building nosec_lines in manager

I'll write a function `_parse_nosec_lines(self, lines, fdata)` that:
1. Tokenizes comments.
2. Collects raw directives: list of `(lineno, kind, selector_text)`.
3. Resolves each selector to suppression value using `nosec.parse_selector`.
4. Computes region lines and next-line target lines.
5. Returns dict.

Let me write it in manager as a method or module function. It needs `lines` for indentation and next-line scanning, and tokenized comments. Let me
keep tokenization inside.

Pseudo:

```python
def _parse_nosec_lines(self, lines):
    nosec_lines = {}
    all_test_ids = nosec.get_all_test_ids()
    # collect directives
    directives = []  # (lineno, kind, selector, indent)
    inline = {}
    try:
        fdata.seek(0)
        tokens = tokenize.tokenize(fdata.readline)
        for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
            if toktype != tokenize.COMMENT:
                continue
            # check end first (so it doesn't get confused as inline nosec)
            if NOSEC_END.search(tokval):
                directives.append((lineno, "end", None, 0))
                continue
            begin = NOSEC_BEGIN.search(tokval)
            if begin:
                directives.append((lineno, "begin", begin.group("selector"), indentation(lines, lineno)))
                continue
            nxt = NOSEC_NEXT.search(tokval)
            if nxt:
                directives.append((lineno, "next", nxt.group("selector"), 0))
                continue
            parsed = _parse_nosec_comment(tokval)
            if parsed is not None:
                inline[lineno] = parsed
    except tokenize.TokenError:
        pass
    ...
```

But `indentation(lines, lineno)` - lines is 0-indexed list, lineno is 1-based. `lines[lineno-1]` is the line. Leading whitespace = `len(line) -
len(line.lstrip())`; for bytes, use lstrip with `b" \t"`.

Now process regions. I need to be careful with auto-end relative to directives on the same line. The region auto-ends "when a later line has smaller
indentation". I'll iterate lines 1..len(lines) and check auto-end before processing directives on that line.

But there's a subtlety: explicit `nosec-end` on a line should end the most recently started active region before the line. If there's also a dedent on
that line, auto-end may have already closed regions. Let's handle:

```python
active_regions = []  # list of dicts: start_line, indent, suppression
regions = []  # completed regions: (start, end_exclusive, suppression)
for lineno in range(1, len(lines)+1):
    indent = indentation(lines, lineno)
    # auto-close active regions whose begin indent > current line indent
    while active_regions and active_regions[-1]["indent"] > indent:
        region = active_regions.pop()
        if region["start_line"] < lineno:
            regions.append((region["start_line"], lineno, region["suppression"]))
    # process directives on this line
    for directive in directives_on_line[lineno]:
        if directive.kind == "begin":
            active_regions.append({...})
        elif directive.kind == "end":
            if active_regions:
                region = active_regions.pop()
                if region["start_line"] < lineno:
                    regions.append((region["start_line"], lineno, region["suppression"]))
                # if start >= lineno, region is empty, discard
# close remaining
for region in active_regions:
    if region["start_line"] <= len(lines):
        regions.append((region["start_line"], len(lines)+1, region["suppression"]))
```

Wait, auto-close with `while active_regions and active_regions[-1]["indent"] > indent` only checks the top. If we have nested regions with indents [8,
4] and current line indent 0, top is 4 > 0 -> pop 4, then top is 8 > 0 -> pop 8. Good. But what about [8, 4] and current line indent 4: top is 4, not
> 4, so no pop. Good. What about [8, 4] and current line indent 6 (weird, dedent from 8 to 6 but 6 > 4 so outer continues): top 4 not > 6, no pop. But
inner region with indent 8 should have been ended when a line with indent 6 came? Wait if current line indent is 6, and inner begin indent was 8, then
6 < 8 so inner should auto-end. But in my stack, top is 4 (outer), not 8 (inner), because inner was pushed after outer, so stack is [8, 4]? Wait push
order: outer begin at indent 4 pushes (indent=4), then inner begin at indent 8 pushes (indent=8). Stack = [outer(4), inner(8)]. Top is inner(8). If
current line indent 6, top 8 > 6 -> pop inner. Good. My earlier example [8,4] would mean outer at 8 then inner at 4? That's not nested correctly;
inner should have >= indent. But if someone puts begin at indent 4 then later begin at indent 4 (same level), stack [outer(4), inner(4)]. Current line
indent 2: top inner(4) > 2 -> pop inner; then top outer(4) > 2 -> pop outer. Good.

But what about non-stack-based auto-end with unusual indent sequences like [outer(4), inner(8)] and current line indent 10? Top inner(8) not > 10, no
pop. Fine.

Now, what about a line with smaller indentation that occurs but there's an explicit nosec-end on that same line? E.g. region begin at indent 4, then a
line at indent 0 with `# nosec-end`. My loop auto-closes at indent 0 before processing the end directive, so the end directive would be "unmatched"
(no active region). But spec says nosec-end ends the most recently started active region before the line. The auto-end should probably take
precedence; the result is the same (region ends at that line). But the explicit end "does nothing" if unmatched - either way region already ended.
Fine. Alternatively, process directives before auto-end? If we process end first, it pops the region and ends it at lineno; then auto-end loop has
nothing to pop. Same outcome. Either way.

Actually there's an ordering concern: if a line has both a dedent (auto-end) and a `nosec-begin` on it, e.g.:
```

        ...
# nosec-begin
```
The second begin at indent 0 should start a new region. If we auto-close before processing directives, the old region closes and then new begin
pushes. Good.

Now, for region suppression values, I need to resolve selector at directive parse time. `parse_selector` returns None (no suppression), set()
(blanket), or set. If selector is `none`, returns None, and the region should have "no effect" — i.e., don't add suppression at all. If it's
empty/`all`, blanket -> empty set. So I'll skip adding regions that resolve to None.

Wait, but for a `nosec-begin none` directive: it has no effect, so we should not create a region at all. Good.

Now the `nosec-next-line` handling:

  But that doesn't handle `...`. Actually `...` is three dots, not in the char class. I'll write a helper:
  ```python
  def _is_skipped_line(line):
      stripped = line.strip()
      if not stripped or stripped.startswith("#"):
          return True
      # remove ellipsis literals
      without_ellipsis = stripped.replace("...", "")
      return re.fullmatch(r"[\s()\[\]{};]*", without_ellipsis) is not None
  ```
  This handles `)...`, `;...`, etc.

  Note: comment-only lines: `stripped.startswith("#")`. But a line like `x = 1 # comment` is not comment-only. Good. A line like `    # comment` is

  Also note: the directive line itself could be a comment-only line. We start scanning from N+1.

- Once T found, add suppression to `nosec_lines[T]` (combining with any existing suppression on that line).

But there's the requirement "Suppress findings for the next statement". A statement might start at a line that is, e.g., `return (foo +` — line
contains `return`, not skipped. Good. What if the next statement is on the same physical line after a semicolon? e.g. `x = 1; # nosec-next-line` then
next statement on same line? Not typical. The directive is at end of line N, and we look at line N+1. Fine.

What if the directive is itself on a line that contains code and the next statement is on the same line after `;`? Not supported; spec says "next
statement after the directive" and scanning lines suggests next physical line.

Now `nosec_lines` building:
- Start with inline nosec dict.
- For each completed region, for line in range(start, end): `nosec_lines[line] = combine(nosec_lines.get(line), region_suppression)`.
- For each next-line directive, `nosec_lines[T] = combine(nosec_lines.get(T), suppression)`.

The `combine` function handles blanket dominance.

```
    bar)
```
The Call node linerange is [1,3], and region covers [1,3]. Adding suppression to blank line 2 doesn't hurt; adding to 1 and 3 does. Fine.

Also, the region "begin takes effect starting on the next line after the directive". So region start = begin_line + 1. And "The directive line itself

Now, what about an explicit `nosec-end` that appears on the same line as code? It ends region before the line containing the directive, so the

### Combining in tester

Now `_get_nosecs_from_contexts` should combine `base_tests` (result.lineno) and `context_tests` (all lines). Since `utils.get_nosec` will now combine
across all lines in linerange, `context_tests` is already combined. But `base_tests` is a single line (result.lineno) which might already be included
in linerange (result.lineno is always the first line of the node's linerange? Usually yes). Actually `result.lineno` is the node's lineno, and
linerange includes it. So `context_tests` from utils.get_nosec already includes result.lineno's suppression if we iterate all lines. Then `base_tests`
is redundant. But to be safe, combine both.

Let me rewrite `_get_nosecs_from_contexts`:

```python
def _get_nosecs_from_contexts(self, context, test_result=None):
    nosec_tests_to_skip = nosec.combine_suppressions([
        self.nosec_lines.get(test_result.lineno) if test_result else None,
        utils.get_nosec(self.nosec_lines, context),
    ])
    return nosec_tests_to_skip
```

Where `nosec.combine_suppressions` takes an iterable and returns None/set()/set. If all None -> None. If any empty set -> set() blanket. Else union.

Actually current `utils.get_nosec` already returns None or a set. Let me just update it to combine across lines, then `_get_nosecs_from_contexts` can
combine base and context.

```python
def get_nosec(nosec_lines, context):
    return nosec.combine_suppressions(
        nosec_lines.get(lineno) for lineno in context["linerange"]
    )
```

### Selector parsing: `_parse_nosec_comment` vs new directive parsing

I'll rewrite `_parse_nosec_comment` to use the new selector parser for the inline nosec's test list, supporting the new expression syntax too. The
spec says "All directive types" and "Each directive accepts an optional selector". Inline `# nosec B602` should also support the new selector syntax?
The issue title is about region/next-line directives, but the selector syntax section applies generally ("Each directive accepts an optional selector
argument"). The existing inline nosec with `B602, B607` should continue to work. It would be nice for inline nosec to support the full expression
syntax too (e.g. `# nosec B602 & B607`). The spec seems to describe the selector language for all directives. Let me support it for inline too.

But there's a compatibility concern: old `NOSEC_COMMENT_TESTS` regex only extracted `B\d+` or `[a-z\d_]+` tokens, ignoring operators. The new parser
can parse `B602, B607` etc. Since `,` and spaces become union, it's backward compatible. `# nosec B607, B602` -> {B602, B607}. Good. `# nosec:
import_subprocess` -> name resolved. Good. `# nosec (on the line)` -> text after nosec is `(on the line)`. The old regex captured `[^#]+` = `(on the
line)`. New parser would see `(` and try to parse expression: `(on the line)` -> tokens `(`, `on`, `the`, `line`, `)`. That would parse as `on | the |
line` (adjacency union). `on`, `the`, `line` are not test names -> empty set -> no suppression! That would break existing examples/nosec.py where
`#nosec (on the line)` is intended as blanket nosec!

Look at examples/nosec.py:
```
subprocess.Popen('/bin/ls *', shell=True) #nosec (on the line)
subprocess.Popen('/bin/ls *', #nosec (at the start of function call)
                 shell=True)
...
subprocess.Popen('/bin/ls *', shell=True)  # nosec subprocess_popen_with_shell_equals_true (on the line)
```

These have human-readable text after the test tokens. The old parser extracted test tokens via regex, ignoring text like `(on the line)`. If I use the
new expression parser on the whole text, it would try to parse `subprocess_popen_with_shell_equals_true (on the line)` as an expression:
`subprocess_popen_with_shell_equals_true | (on | the | line)` — `on`, `the`, `line` are unknown -> resolve to empty; union with the valid name ->
still valid. That works! Because union with empty set is just the valid set. But for pure `(on the line)` with no valid token, it would resolve to
empty set -> no suppression, breaking `#nosec (on the line)` as blanket.

Hmm. The old behavior: `_parse_nosec_comment("#nosec (on the line)")` — `NOSEC_COMMENT` matches with tests = `(on the line)`.
`NOSEC_COMMENT_TESTS.finditer` on `(on the line)` finds `on`, `the`, `line` (since the regex matches word sequences). None are test names ->
`test_ids` stays empty -> returns `set()` (blanket). So the comment with unrecognized text still suppresses everything (blanket).

So there's an important semantic: if the nosec comment has text but none of it resolves to known tests, it's still blanket? Actually the current
behavior: any `# nosec` without recognizable test IDs/names results in an empty set -> blanket. So unrecognized tokens are ignored, and if no valid
tests, it's blanket. That means `# nosec B602, B607` -> {B602,B607}, `# nosec B999` -> empty set -> blanket! That's a bit odd but it's the current
behavior.

For the new selector language, the spec says "If the expression cannot be parsed, fall back to treating all whitespace and comma-separated tokens as a
plain union." It doesn't say what to do with unknown test tokens. The `none` keyword is the explicit "no effect". So unknown tokens probably resolve
to no tests (empty set), making the directive no effect. But that contradicts old behavior for `# nosec B999` (old: blanket). However, the old
behavior might not be a hard requirement; the issue is about adding new directives. Let me think about what upstream does.

Actually, I recall upstream's behavior: for inline `# nosec`, the new selector parser is used. If the selector cannot be parsed, fallback to union of
tokens. Unknown tokens... Let me check the spec: "Tokens may be test IDs or test names. Test IDs may include a glob wildcard..." It doesn't say
unknown tokens are blanket. I think for the new implementation, unknown tokens just don't match any test, resulting in empty set -> no suppression.
But then the existing examples with `(on the line)` would break because `(on the line)` is a parenthesized expression that parses successfully but
resolves to empty set.

STEP 33 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
These are plain `# nosec` and `# nosec B608`, which the new parser handles fine.

Let me now implement. I'll start by writing the new `bandit/core/nosec.py` module.

Let me write the parser carefully.

```python
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
import fnmatch
import logging
import re

from bandit.core import extension_loader

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)

BLANKET = "all"
NONE = "none"

_TOKEN_RE = re.compile(
    r"\s*(\(|\)|\||&|-|!|,|[A-Za-z0-9_*.]+)"
)


class Node:
    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        raise NotImplementedError


class All(Node):
    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        return BLANKET


class Nothing(Node):
    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        return NONE


class Test(Node):
    def __init__(self, value):
        self.value = value

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        matches = set()
        value = self.value
        if value == "all":
            return BLANKET
        if value == "none":
            return NONE
        if "*" in value:
            for test_id in all_tests:
                if fnmatch.fnmatchcase(test_id, value):
                    matches.add(test_id)
            return matches
        if value in all_tests:
            matches.add(value)
            return matches
        test_id = extension_loader.MANAGER.get_test_id(value)
        if test_id and test_id in all_tests:
            matches.add(test_id)
        return matches
```

Wait, but in the grammar, `all` and `none` should be treated as special atoms. I'll handle them in the parser as All/Nothing nodes, not as Test.
That's cleaner. But also `all` could appear as a token in an expression like `all & B602`. I'll parse `all` as All node and `none` as Nothing node.

`Test.resolve`:
- If value has `*`, match all test IDs with glob.
- Else if value in all_tests -> that ID.
- Else try `extension_loader.MANAGER.get_test_id(value)` (test name) and if that ID in all_tests -> add.
- Else return empty set.

Now the operator nodes:

```python
class Union(Node):
    def __init__(self, left, right):
        self.left = left
        self.right = right

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        left = self.left.resolve(all_tests)
        right = self.right.resolve(all_tests)
        if left is BLANKET or right is BLANKET:
            return BLANKET
        left = set() if left is NONE else set(left)
        right = set() if right is NONE else set(right)
        return left | right


class Intersection(Node):
    def __init__(self, left, right):
        self.left = left
        self.right = right

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        left = self.left.resolve(all_tests)
        right = self.right.resolve(all_tests)
        if left is NONE or right is NONE:
            return NONE
        if left is BLANKET:
            return right
        if right is BLANKET:
            return left
        return left & right


class Difference(Node):
    def __init__(self, left, right):
        self.left = left
        self.right = right

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        left = self.left.resolve(all_tests)
        right = self.right.resolve(all_tests)
        if left is NONE:
            return NONE
        if right is NONE:
            return left
        if left is BLANKET:
            if right is BLANKET:
                return NONE
            return set(all_tests) - set(right)
        if right is BLANKET:
            return NONE
        return set(left) - set(right)


class Negation(Node):
    def __init__(self, expr):
        self.expr = expr

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        inner = self.expr.resolve(all_tests)
        if inner is NONE:
            return BLANKET
        if inner is BLANKET:
            return NONE
        return set(all_tests) - set(inner)
```

Parser:

```python
class SelectorParser:
    def __init__(self, text):
        self.text = text
        self.tokens = self._tokenize(text)
        self.pos = 0

    def _tokenize(self, text):
        tokens = []
        for match in _TOKEN_RE.finditer(text):
            token = match.group(1)
            if token == ",":
                tokens.append("|")
            else:
                tokens.append(token)
        # insert union for adjacency
        result = []
        prev = None
        for token in tokens:
            if result:
                prev = result[-1]
                if _is_operand_end(prev) and _is_operand_start(token):
                    result.append("|")
            result.append(token)
        return result
```

Where `_is_operand_end(token)` returns True if token is a word or `)`; `_is_operand_start(token)` returns True if token is a word or `(`.

Wait, but after inserting `|`, the token sequence has operators. For example `B602 B607` -> tokens `['B602', '|', 'B607']`. `B602 (on the line)` ->
`['B602', '|', '(', 'on', '|', 'the', '|', 'line', ')']` -> `B602 | (on | the | line)`.

But what about `! B602`? Tokens `['!', 'B602']` - no adjacency insertion because `!` is not operand end. Good. `B602 ! B607`? That's weird (`B602 !
B607`); `!` is unary prefix, but after `B602` it would be `B602` followed by `!` which isn't operand start, so no `|` inserted. Then parser would fail
on `!` in infix position. Fallback to plain union. Fine.

`(B602 B607)` -> `['(', 'B602', '|', 'B607', ')']`.

Now recursive descent:

```python
    def parse(self):
        if not self.tokens:
            return All()
        node = self.parse_union()
        if self.pos != len(self.tokens):
            raise ValueError("Unexpected token: %s" % self.tokens[self.pos])
        return node

    def parse_union(self):
        left = self.parse_intersection()
        while self._peek() == "|":
            self._advance()
            right = self.parse_intersection()
            left = Union(left, right)
        return left

    def parse_intersection(self):
        left = self.parse_difference()
        while self._peek() == "&":
            self._advance()
            right = self.parse_difference()
            left = Intersection(left, right)
        return left

    def parse_difference(self):
        left = self.parse_unary()
        while self._peek() == "-":
            self._advance()
            right = self.parse_unary()
            left = Difference(left, right)
        return left

    def parse_unary(self):
        if self._peek() == "!":
            self._advance()
            return Negation(self.parse_unary())
        return self.parse_primary()

    def parse_primary(self):
        token = self._advance()
        if token == "(":
            node = self.parse_union()
            if self._advance() != ")":
                raise ValueError("Expected )")
            return node
        if token == ")":
            raise ValueError("Unexpected )")
        if token in ("|", "&", "-", "!"):
            raise ValueError("Unexpected operator")
        if token.lower() == "all":
            return All()
        if token.lower() == "none":
            return Nothing()
        return Test(token)
```

But there's an issue: `_peek` after `(B602)` — parse_primary calls parse_union which consumes until `)`; then parse_union in outer context will see
`)` and stop; then parse_primary checks `)` and returns. Good.

Also, what about a trailing `|` like `B602 |`? parse_union: after parsing left, peek `|`, advance, parse_intersection -> parse_primary -> advance
returns `|` (end) -> error. Good, fallback.

Now the "fallback to plain union of whitespace/comma tokens" if parse fails. I'll implement `parse_selector(text, all_tests)`:

```python
def parse_selector(text):
    """Parse a selector string into a suppression value.

    Returns:
        None for no suppression (none), set() for blanket (all),
        or a non-empty set of test IDs.
    """
    if text is None:
        text = ""
    text = text.strip()
    try:
        node = SelectorParser(text).parse()
    except (ValueError, IndexError):
        node = _fallback_union(text)
    value = node.resolve(get_all_test_ids())
    return _to_suppression(value)


def _fallback_union(text):
    if not text.strip():
        return All()
    node = Nothing()
    for token in re.split(r"[\s,]+", text.strip()):
        if not token:
            continue
        if token.lower() == "all":
            return All()
        if token.lower() == "none":
            continue
        node = Union(node, Test(token))
    return node


def _to_suppression(value):
    if value is BLANKET:
        return set()
    if value is NONE:
        return None
    return set(value)  # may be empty -> None handled by caller?
```

Hmm, if `_to_suppression` returns an empty set for value == NONE? Wait value is NONE sentinel for empty. For a set value that's empty (e.g. expression
`B602 & B607` where no overlap), `resolve` returns an empty set, not NONE. Should that be None (no suppression) or empty set (blanket)? We must not
return empty set here because empty set means blanket! So `_to_suppression` must convert empty set to None. But what if the expression legitimately
resolves to empty and the user wants "no tests"? That's the same as none -> no suppression. So:

```python
def _to_suppression(value):
    if value is BLANKET:
        return set()
    if value is NONE:
        return None
    value = set(value)
    if not value:
        return None
    return value
```

that per spec? It says tokens may be test IDs or names; unknown tokens aren't defined. I think no effect is the right interpretation (not blanket).

blanket. Good.

#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Nosec selector expression parsing and suppression resolution.

Suppression directives (``# nosec``, ``# nosec-begin`` and
``# nosec-next-line``) may specify which tests are suppressed using a small
expression language.  This module parses that language and resolves the parsed
expression against the set of tests bandit knows about.
"""

import fnmatch
import logging
import re

from bandit.core import extension_loader

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# Sentinel returned by an expression when it resolves to a blanket
# suppression (all tests are suppressed).
BLANKET = "all"
# Sentinel returned by an expression when it resolves to an empty selection
# (no tests are suppressed).
NONE = "none"

_TOKEN_RE = re.compile(r"\s*(\(|\)|\||&|-|!|,|[A-Za-z0-9_*.]+)")


def get_all_test_ids():
    """Return the set of all test IDs known to bandit."""
    extman = extension_loader.MANAGER
    test_ids = set(extman.plugins_by_id.keys())
    test_ids.update(extman.blacklist_by_id.keys())
    test_ids.update(extman.builtin)
    return test_ids


def combine(suppressions):
    """Combine a sequence of suppression values into one.

    Suppression values are ``None`` (no suppression), ``set()`` (blanket
    suppression) or a non-empty set of test IDs (specific suppression).  A
    blanket suppression dominates all other suppressions; otherwise the
    specific suppressions are unioned.

    :param suppressions: iterable of suppression values
    :return: ``None``, ``set()`` or a non-empty set of test IDs
    """
    result = None
    for suppression in suppressions:
        if suppression is None:
            continue
        if not suppression:
            # Empty set means a blanket suppression: all tests are skipped.
            return set()
        if result is None:
            result = set()
        result.update(suppression)
    return result


class Node:
    """Base class for parsed selector expressions."""

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        raise NotImplementedError


class All(Node):
    """Matches every test (blanket suppression)."""

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        return BLANKET


class Nothing(Node):
    """Matches no tests."""

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        return NONE


class Test(Node):
    """A single test ID, test name, or glob pattern."""

    def __init__(self, value):
        self.value = value

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        matches = set()
        value = self.value
        if "*" in value:
            for test_id in all_tests:
                if fnmatch.fnmatchcase(test_id, value):
                    matches.add(test_id)
            return matches
        if value in all_tests:
            matches.add(value)
            return matches
        test_id = extension_loader.MANAGER.get_test_id(value)
        if test_id and test_id in all_tests:
            matches.add(test_id)
        return matches


class Union(Node):
    def __init__(self, left, right):
        self.left = left
        self.right = right

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        left = self.left.resolve(all_tests)
        right = self.right.resolve(all_tests)
        if left is BLANKET or right is BLANKET:
            return BLANKET
        left = set() if left is NONE else set(left)
        right = set() if right is NONE else set(right)
        return left | right


class Intersection(Node):
    def __init__(self, left, right):
        self.left = left
        self.right = right

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        left = self.left.resolve(all_tests)
        right = self.right.resolve(all_tests)
        if left is NONE or right is NONE:
            return NONE
        if left is BLANKET:
            return right
        if right is BLANKET:
            return left
        return left & right


class Difference(Node):
    def __init__(self, left, right):
        self.left = left
        self.right = right

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        left = self.left.resolve(all_tests)
        right = self.right.resolve(all_tests)
        if left is NONE:
            return NONE
        if right is NONE:
            return left
        if left is BLANKET:
            if right is BLANKET:
                return NONE
            return set(all_tests) - set(right)
        if right is BLANKET:
            return NONE
        return set(left) - set(right)


class Negation(Node):
    def __init__(self, expr):
        self.expr = expr

    def resolve(self, all_tests):
        inner = self.expr.resolve(all_tests)
        if inner is NONE:
            return BLANKET
        if inner is BLANKET:
            return NONE
        return set(all_tests) - set(inner)


def _is_operand_end(token):
    return token not in ("(", ")", "|", "&", "-", "!", ",")


def _is_operand_start(token):
    return token not in ("(", ")", "|", "&", "-", "!", ",")


class SelectorParser:
    """Recursive descent parser for selector expressions.

    Grammar (lowest to highest precedence)::

        union         := intersection (('|') intersection)*
        intersection  := difference ('&' difference)*
        difference    := unary ('-' unary)*
        unary         := '!' unary | primary
        primary       := '(' union ')' | atom

    Tokens separated by whitespace or commas are treated as unions, so
    ``B602 B607`` is equivalent to ``B602 | B607``.
    """

    def __init__(self, text):
        self.tokens = self._tokenize(text)
        self.pos = 0

    @staticmethod
    def _tokenize(text):
        tokens = []
        for match in _TOKEN_RE.finditer(text):
            token = match.group(1)
            if token == ",":
                token = "|"
            tokens.append(token)
        # Insert explicit union operators between adjacent operands.
        result = []
        for token in tokens:
            if result and _is_operand_end(result[-1]) and _is_operand_start(
                token
            ):
                result.append("|")
            result.append(token)
        return result

    def parse(self):
        if not self.tokens:
            return All()
        node = self.parse_union()
        if self.pos != len(self.tokens):
            raise ValueError(
                "Unexpected token in selector: %s" % self.tokens[self.pos]
            )
        return node

    def parse_union(self):
        left = self.parse_intersection()
        while self._peek() == "|":
            self._advance()
            right = self.parse_intersection()
            left = Union(left, right)
        return left

    def parse_intersection(self):
        left = self.parse_difference()
        while self._peek() == "&":
            self._advance()
            right = self.parse_difference()
            left = Intersection(left, right)
        return left

    def parse_difference(self):
        left = self.parse_unary()
        while self._peek() == "-":
            self._advance()
            right = self.parse_unary()
            left = Difference(left, right)
        return left

    def parse_unary(self):
        if self._peek() == "!":
            self._advance()
            return Negation(self.parse_unary())
        return self.parse_primary()

    def parse_primary(self):
        token = self._advance()
        if token is None:
            raise ValueError("Unexpected end of selector")
        if token == "(":
            node = self.parse_union()
            closing = self._advance()
            if closing != ")":
                raise ValueError("Expected ')' in selector")
            return node
        if token in ("|", "&", "-", "!", ")", ","):
            raise ValueError("Unexpected token in selector: %s" % token)
        lower = token.lower()
        if lower == "all":
            return All()
        if lower == "none":
            return Nothing()
        return Test(token)

    def _peek(self):
        if self.pos >= len(self.tokens):
            return None
        return self.tokens[self.pos]

    def _advance(self):
        if self.pos >= len(self.tokens):
            return None
        token = self.tokens[self.pos]
        self.pos += 1
        return token


def _fallback_union(text):
    """Parse an invalid expression as a plain union of tokens."""
    if not text.strip():
        return All()
    node = Nothing()
    for token in re.split(r"[\s,]+", text.strip()):
        if not token:
            continue
        lower = token.lower()
        if lower == "all":
            return All()
        if lower == "none":
            continue
        node = Union(node, Test(token))
    return node


def _to_suppression(value):
    """Convert a resolved expression value to a suppression value."""
    if value is BLANKET:
        return set()
    if value is NONE:
        return None
    value = set(value)
    if not value:
        return None
    return value


def parse_selector(text):
    """Parse a selector string into a suppression value.

    :param text: selector text, possibly empty or ``None``
    :return: ``None`` for no suppression, ``set()`` for a blanket
        suppression, or a non-empty set of test IDs for a specific
        suppression
    """
    if text is None:
        text = ""
    text = text.strip()
    try:
        node = SelectorParser(text).parse()
    except (ValueError, IndexError):
        LOG.debug("Could not parse nosec selector %r, falling back to union", text)
        node = _fallback_union(text)
    return _to_suppression(node.resolve(get_all_test_ids()))

        :param config: config options object
        :type config: bandit.core.BanditConfig
        :param agg_type: aggregation type
        :param debug: Whether to show debug messages or not
        :param verbose: Whether to show verbose output
        :param quiet: Whether to only show output in the case of an error
        :param profile_name: Optional name of profile to use (from cmd line)
        :param ignore_nosec: Whether to ignore #nosec or not
        :return:
        """

    def get_skipped(self):
        ret = []
        # "skip" is a tuple of name and reason, decode just the name
        for skip in self.skipped:
            if isinstance(skip[0], bytes):

    def _parse_file(self, fname, fdata, new_files_list):
        try:
            # parse the current file
            data = fdata.read()
            lines = data.splitlines()
            self.metrics.begin(fname)
            self.metrics.count_locs(lines)
            # nosec_lines is a dict of line number -> set of tests to ignore
            #                                         for the line
            nosec_lines = dict()
            try:
                fdata.seek(0)
                tokens = tokenize.tokenize(fdata.readline)

                if not self.ignore_nosec:
                    for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
                        if toktype == tokenize.COMMENT:
                            nosec_lines[lineno] = _parse_nosec_comment(tokval)

            except tokenize.TokenError:
                pass
            score = self._execute_ast_visitor(fname, fdata, data, nosec_lines)
            self.scores.append(score)
            self.metrics.count_issues([score])
        except KeyboardInterrupt:
            sys.exit(2)
        except SyntaxError:
            self.skipped.append(
                (fname, "syntax error while parsing AST from file")
            )
            new_files_list.remove(fname)
        except Exception as e:
            LOG.error(
                "Exception occurred when executing tests against %s.", fname
            )
            if not LOG.isEnabledFor(logging.DEBUG):
                LOG.error(
                    'Run "bandit --debug %s" to see the full traceback.', fname
                )

            self.skipped.append((fname, "exception while scanning file"))
            new_files_list.remove(fname)
            LOG.debug("  Exception string: %s", e)
            LOG.debug("  Exception traceback: %s", traceback.format_exc())

    def _execute_ast_visitor(self, fname, fdata, data, nosec_lines):
        """Execute AST parse on each file

        :param fname: The name of the file being parsed
        :param data: Original file contents
        :param lines: The lines of code to process
        :return: The accumulated test score
        """
        score = []
        res = b_node_visitor.BanditNodeVisitor(
            fname,
            fdata,
            self.b_ma,
            self.b_ts,
            self.debug,
            nosec_lines,
            self.metrics,
        )

        score = res.process(data)
        self.results.extend(res.tester.results)
        return score

    files_dir, included_globs=None, excluded_path_strings=None
):
    if not included_globs:
        included_globs = ["*.py"]

def _find_test_id_from_nosec_string(extman, match):
    test_id = extman.check_id(match)
    if test_id:
        return match
    # Finding by short_id didn't work, let's check the test name
    test_id = extman.get_test_id(match)
    if not test_id:
        # Name and short id didn't work:
        LOG.warning(
            "Test in comment: %s is not a test name or id, ignoring", match
        )
    return test_id  # We want to return None or the string here regardless


def _parse_nosec_comment(comment):
    found_no_sec_comment = NOSEC_COMMENT.search(comment)
    if not found_no_sec_comment:
        # there was no nosec comment
        return None

    matches = found_no_sec_comment.groupdict()
    nosec_tests = matches.get("tests", set())

    # empty set indicates that there was a nosec comment without specific
    # test ids or names
    test_ids = set()
    if nosec_tests:
        extman = extension_loader.MANAGER
        # lookup tests by short code or name
        for test in NOSEC_COMMENT_TESTS.finditer(nosec_tests):
            test_match = test.group(1)
            test_id = _find_test_id_from_nosec_string(extman, test_match)
            if test_id:
                test_ids.add(test_id)

    return test_ids

```python
NOSEC_COMMENT = re.compile(
    r"#\s*nosec(?![-\w])\s*:?\s*(?P<tests>[^#]+)?#?", re.IGNORECASE

NOSEC_BEGIN_COMMENT = re.compile(
    r"#\s*nosec-begin\b\s*:?\s*(?P<selector>[^#]*?)\s*(?:#|$)", re.IGNORECASE

NOSEC_END_COMMENT = re.compile(r"#\s*nosec-end\b", re.IGNORECASE)
```

For `# nosec B602, B607`, selector text = "B602, B607" -> {B602,B607}. Good.

But there's a subtlety with the old regex capturing trailing `#` etc. `# nosec # noqa`: the regex `#\s*nosec(?![-\w])\s*:?\s*(?P<tests>[^#]+)?#?` —
after `nosec` there's a space, `(?![-\w])` ok. Then `\s*` consumes spaces. `:?` optional. `(?P<tests>[^#]+)?` — next char is `#`, so `[^#]+` fails;
optional group is None. Then `#?` matches the `#`. So tests = None -> blanket. Good.

For `# nosec B607 # noqa`: `[^#]+` matches `B607 ` until `#`. tests = "B607 " -> parse_selector -> {B607}. Good.

```python
def _parse_nosec_lines(self, lines):
    """Build a mapping of line number -> suppression from comments.

    Handles inline ``# nosec``, region directives (``# nosec-begin`` /
    ``# nosec-end``) and ``# nosec-next-line``.
    """

    directives = []  # (lineno, kind, selector, indent)

    def _leading_whitespace(line):
```

Actually `_parse_file` has `fdata`. I'll pass `fdata` to the method. Or do tokenization in `_parse_file` and pass a list of comments. Let me write a
method `_parse_nosec_lines(self, lines, fdata)`:

```python
def _parse_nosec_lines(self, lines, fdata):
    nosec_lines = {}
    directives = []  # (lineno, kind, selector, indent)
    try:
        fdata.seek(0)
        tokens = tokenize.tokenize(fdata.readline)
        for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
            if toktype != tokenize.COMMENT:
                continue
            if NOSEC_END_COMMENT.search(tokval):
                directives.append((lineno, "end", None, 0))
                continue
            begin = NOSEC_BEGIN_COMMENT.search(tokval)
            if begin:
                directives.append(
                    (lineno, "begin", begin.group("selector"),
                     _leading_whitespace(lines[lineno - 1]))
                )
                continue
            nxt = NOSEC_NEXT_COMMENT.search(tokval)
            if nxt:
                directives.append(
                    (lineno, "next", nxt.group("selector"), 0)
                )
                continue
            parsed = _parse_nosec_comment(tokval)
            if parsed is not None:
                nosec_lines[lineno] = b_nosec.combine(
                    [nosec_lines.get(lineno), parsed]
                )
    except tokenize.TokenError:
        pass

    # Build region suppressions.
    active_regions = []
    completed_regions = []
    by_line = {}
    for d in directives:
        by_line.setdefault(d[0], []).append(d)

    for lineno in range(1, len(lines) + 1):
        indent = _leading_whitespace(lines[lineno - 1])
        # Auto-close regions that ended by dedentation.
        while active_regions and active_regions[-1][1] > indent:
            region = active_regions.pop()
            start, begin_indent, suppression = region
            if start < lineno:
                completed_regions.append((start, lineno, suppression))
        for _, kind, selector, _ in by_line.get(lineno, []):
            if kind == "begin":
                suppression = b_nosec.parse_selector(selector)
                if suppression is not None:
                    active_regions.append((lineno + 1, indent, suppression))
            elif kind == "end":
                if active_regions:
                    start, _, suppression = active_regions.pop()
                    if start < lineno:
                        completed_regions.append((start, lineno, suppression))
            # 'next' handled separately below
    for start, _, suppression in active_regions:
        if start <= len(lines):
            completed_regions.append((start, len(lines) + 1, suppression))

    for start, end, suppression in completed_regions:
        for lineno in range(start, end):
            nosec_lines[lineno] = b_nosec.combine(
                [nosec_lines.get(lineno), suppression]
            )

    # Build next-line suppressions.
    for lineno, kind, selector, _ in directives:
        if kind != "next":
            continue
        suppression = b_nosec.parse_selector(selector)
        if suppression is None:
            continue
        target = _find_next_statement_line(lines, lineno)
        if target is not None:
            nosec_lines[target] = b_nosec.combine(
                [nosec_lines.get(target), suppression]
            )

    return nosec_lines
```

And helper `_find_next_statement_line(lines, lineno)`:
```python
def _find_next_statement_line(lines, lineno):
    for idx in range(lineno, len(lines)):
        line = lines[idx]
        if _is_skip_line(line):
            continue
        return idx + 1  # convert 0-based index to 1-based line number
    return None
```

Wait, `lines` is 0-indexed. `lineno` is 1-based. The directive is on line `lineno`. We scan lines from index `lineno` (which is line `lineno+1`) to
end. If line at index i is not skipped, return i+1.

`_is_skip_line`:
```python
def _is_skip_line(line):
    if isinstance(line, bytes):
        stripped = line.strip(b" \t")
        if not stripped or stripped.startswith(b"#"):
            return True
        no_ellipsis = stripped.replace(b"...", b"")
        return re.fullmatch(rb"[\s()\[\]{};]*", no_ellipsis) is not None
    stripped = line.strip()
    if not stripped or stripped.startswith("#"):
        return True
    no_ellipsis = stripped.replace("...", "")
    return re.fullmatch(r"[\s()\[\]{};]*", no_ellipsis) is not None
```

Wait, after `.strip()`, whitespace is already removed, so `[\s()\[\]{};]*` would only match grouping chars. But I need to allow whitespace within?
After strip, no leading/trailing whitespace, but there could be internal whitespace like `( )` -> after strip `( )`, no_ellipsis = `( )`. `[\s...]*`
matches `( )`. Good.

Now, one issue: the auto-close loop uses `active_regions[-1][1] > indent`, where region tuple is `(start, indent, suppression)`. But if there are
multiple regions with the same indentation, and current line has the same indent, we don't close. Good.

But what about nested regions where an inner region has the same indent as outer (sibling)? E.g.:
```
if x:
    # nosec-begin  (indent 4)
    foo()
    # nosec-begin  (indent 4)
    bar()
```
Stack: [outer(4), inner(4)]. A line with indent 4: top inner(4) not > 4, so no close. But semantically, the second begin at the same indent probably
should be nested? Actually two begins at the same indentation level: the first region covers from its next line until... the second begin starts
another region inside the first. That's weird. `nosec-end` pops the most recent (inner). Auto-end by indent would close inner when dedent below 4, and
then outer would also close (since both have indent 4). Fine. Not a big concern.

Now, the order of processing directives on a line: I iterate `by_line.get(lineno, [])` in the order directives were appended (which is token order).
Good.

Now, there's a subtle issue with auto-close when a line has smaller indentation but is inside a region and also has a `nosec-begin` at the smaller
indentation. Example:
```
def f():
    # nosec-begin
    foo()
# nosec-begin
bar()
```
Line 4 (indent 0) is `# nosec-begin`. Auto-close first: active region (indent 4) -> close at line 4. Then process begin -> push new region start line
5. Good.

Now, `_parse_file` will call `self._parse_nosec_lines(lines, fdata)` and use its return for `nosec_lines`. If `ignore_nosec`, we pass empty dict
(current behavior: if ignore_nosec, loop doesn't run so nosec_lines stays empty). I'll keep that: `nosec_lines = {}` if ignore_nosec else
`self._parse_nosec_lines(...)`.

But wait: the current code tokenizes once for both. My method tokenizes again (calls fdata.seek(0)). That's fine.

Now, `_execute_ast_visitor` currently passes `nosec_lines`. No change needed there.

        """Build the nosec line mapping from all suppression directives.

        Handles inline ``# nosec`` comments, ``# nosec-begin`` /
        ``# nosec-end`` region directives and ``# nosec-next-line``
        directives.

        :param lines: split lines of the file (bytes)
        :param fdata: file data stream
        :return: dict mapping line numbers to suppression values
        """
        nosec_lines = {}
        directives = []  # (lineno, kind, selector, indent)

        def leading_whitespace(line):
            return len(line) - len(line.lstrip(b" \\t"))

        try:
            fdata.seek(0)
            tokens = tokenize.tokenize(fdata.readline)
            for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
                if toktype != tokenize.COMMENT:

                if NOSEC_END_COMMENT.search(tokval):
                    directives.append((lineno, "end", None, 0))

                begin = NOSEC_BEGIN_COMMENT.search(tokval)

                if parsed is not None:
                    nosec_lines[lineno] = b_nosec.combine(
                        [nosec_lines.get(lineno), parsed]

        except tokenize.TokenError:

        # Process region directives.
        active_regions = []  # (start_line, indent, suppression)
        completed_regions = []  # (start_line, end_line, suppression)
        directives_by_line = {}
        for directive in directives:
            directives_by_line.setdefault(directive[0], []).append(directive)

        for lineno in range(1, len(lines) + 1):
            indent = leading_whitespace(lines[lineno - 1])
            # Automatically end regions when a later line has smaller
            # indentation than the line containing the begin directive.

                if start < lineno:

new_tail = '''def _is_skip_line(line):
    """Return True for lines skipped when locating a next statement.

    Blank lines, comment-only lines, and lines containing only grouping
    tokens, semicolons or ellipsis literals are skipped.
    """
    if isinstance(line, bytes):
        stripped = line.strip(b" \\t")
        if not stripped or stripped.startswith(b"#"):
            return True
        no_ellipsis = stripped.replace(b"...", b"")

    stripped = line.strip()

    no_ellipsis = stripped.replace("...", "")
    return re.fullmatch(r"[\\s()\\[\\]{};]*", no_ellipsis) is not None


def _find_next_statement_line(lines, lineno):
    """Find the first non-skipped line after the given line number."""
    for idx in range(lineno, len(lines)):
        if not _is_skip_line(lines[idx]):
            return idx + 1
    return None

NOSEC_COMMENT = re.compile(
    r"#\\s*nosec(?![\\w-])\\s*:?\\s*(?P<tests>[^#]*?)\\s*(?:#|$)",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)
NOSEC_BEGIN_COMMENT = re.compile(
    r"#\\s*nosec-begin\\b\\s*:?\\s*(?P<selector>[^#]*?)\\s*(?:#|$)",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)
NOSEC_END_COMMENT = re.compile(r"#\\s*nosec-end\\b", re.IGNORECASE)
NOSEC_NEXT_COMMENT = re.compile(
    r"#\\s*nosec-next-line\\b\\s*:?\\s*(?P<selector>[^#]*?)\\s*(?:#|$)",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)
PROGRESS_THRESHOLD = 50'''

            #                                         for the line
            nosec_lines = dict()
            try:
                fdata.seek(0)
                tokens = tokenize.tokenize(fdata.readline)

                if not self.ignore_nosec:
                    for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
                        if toktype == tokenize.COMMENT:
                            nosec_lines[lineno] = _parse_nosec_comment(tokval)
